Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `dsp48e2_macc` (precision INT27)
- what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, "27x18 + C MULT/MACC, one slice" (UG579 v1.7 Table 2-12 (p44); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p28)). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
- computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: `OPMODE` chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
- input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
- output ports: P[48]
- arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 p36: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on `A` and 17 bits on `B`; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: on `CLK`, the block has exactly these registers. `A2` loads A at a clock edge where `CEA2` is high, and is cleared to 0 by `RSTA`. `B2` loads B at a clock edge where `CEB2` is high, and is cleared to 0 by `RSTB`. `P` loads the value OPMODE selects from A2*B2, C and P at a clock edge where `CEP` is high, and is cleared to 0 by `RSTP`; the output `P` is this register. The block has no path around a register.
- timing: `A` and `B` reach `P` 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in `P` after edge k+1); `C` and `OPMODE` have no register before the one that drives `P`: the value present at a clock edge is in `P` after that edge (1 cycle).
- control `OPMODE`: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:
  - 000000101 (multiply): P <= A2*B2
  - 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + A2*B2
  - 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + A2*B2
  - 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + A2*B2
  - 110000000 (load C): P <= C
  - 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
  - 010000000 (hold): P <= P
  - 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
  - any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
- resets and clock enables (resets `RSTA`, `RSTB` and `RSTP`; clock enables `CEA2`, `CEB2` and `CEP`): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
- power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so `P` is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; `OPMODE` may be a constant legal value.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, tpu_like.small.ws.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, "27x18 + C MULT/MACC, one slice" (UG579 v1.7 Table 2-12 (p44); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p28)). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 p36: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. P loads the value OPMODE selects from A2*B2, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. The block has no path around a register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+1); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= A2*B2
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + A2*B2
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + A2*B2
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + A2*B2
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2 and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at A, B, RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2 and CEP: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at C and OPMODE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

Multiplies in the unmodified tpu_like.small.ws, counted in its packed netlist at seed 1: 290 on 113 DSP tiles, of which 289 in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode on 112 tiles (44 tiles with 4, 10 with 3, 25 with 2 and 33 with 1) and 1 in two_mult_18x19 mode on 1 tiles (1 to a tile). 50 products in the source become 290 multiply cells after synthesis. The DSP tile count is the same at each of the five seeds. Multiplies in soft logic, if any, are not in this count.

Each row is one multiply of the design as elaborated from the source. An id names it by its module and by the name the elaborated design gives the multiply, which carries the file and the line; multiplies that come from one line (several written on it, or one inside a generate loop) differ in the number after the last $. An operand's bits are the bits it carries, the zero or sign extension to the width of the expression left out; signed or unsigned is how the multiply reads it. Instances is how many instances of the multiply's module the design holds. Nothing else about a multiply is listed: what its product feeds is in the source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2 and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Goal

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.

No rewrite
  • REGIONS: NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or nothing left to try. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the REGIONS line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_ws_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (dsp48e2_macc.v) are in sim_reference/.

response

I'll start by reading the design source and the relevant regions.

REGIONS: sub_activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.ws.v:8509$1681
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 16
MAPPING: one dsp48e2_macc per sub_activation instance (16 instances, all inside module activation): CLK=clk; A={19'b0, data_slope} (8-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 27, MSB 0); B={10'b0, inp_data} (8-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 18, MSB 0); C={40'b0, data_intercept_delayed} (kept as a soft register, unchanged); OPMODE= (activation_type==1'b1) ? 9'b000110101 (C + A2*B2) : 9'b010000000 (hold); RSTA=RSTB=RSTP= act_clr = ((reset | ~enable_activation) | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress)); CEA2=CEB2= activation_type; CEP=1'b1; P[7:0] replaces the register intercept_applied_data_internal (the block's A2/B2 pair replaces the register slope_applied_data_internal).

PLAN:

What is converted and why it is exact

Only the tanh multiply in sub_activation (line 8509) is converted. In that module the product feeds a register and that register feeds an adder into a second register, which is exactly the block's A2/B2-then-P shape:

Design (cycle n values; clr = (reset|~enable_activation) | ~(in_data_available|activation_in_progress), tanh = activation_type==1):

  • slope_applied_data_internal[n+1] = clr ? 0 : (tanh ? (data_slope*inp_data) mod 256 : hold) (lines 8498, 8509, 8545)
  • intercept_applied_data_internal[n+1] = clr ? 0 : (tanh ? (slope_applied_data_internal + data_intercept_delayed) mod 256 : hold) (lines 8499, 8511, 8546)

Block, with the mapping above:

  • A2[n+1]/B2[n+1] = clr ? 0 : (tanh ? data_slope/inp_data : hold) — RSTA/RSTB win over CEA2/CEB2, so the three cases match one-for-one. By induction from power-up (both the block's registers and, under the testbench's $init_zero, the design's registers start at 0), (A2*B2) mod 2^8 == slope_applied_data_internal on every cycle; the design truncates the product to 8 bits and only its low 8 bits are ever used downstream, so the full 45-bit product is a legal stand-in.
  • P[n+1] = clr ? 0 (RSTP) : (tanh ? C + A2*B2 : P) — so P[7:0] == intercept_applied_data_internal on every cycle, because the design's add is mod 2^8 and low-8 addition only depends on low-8 operands.

Visibility: slope_applied_data_internal is read only at line 8511 (checked: declarations/uses at 8480, 8498, 8509, 8511, 8545), so it may disappear inside the block as the A2/B2 pair. intercept_applied_data_internal is read only at line 8557, which now reads P[7:0]. data_intercept_delayed stays a soft register (its own update at lines 8501/8510/8548 is untouched) and drives C; C is not registered in the block, so the value at cycle n+1 is the one added at edge n+2 — the same cycle the design uses it.

No assumption is needed: every control signal (reset, enable_activation, in_data_available, activation_in_progress, activation_type) is sampled in the same cycle the original samples it, so the mapping holds after every mid-run reset of reset, after enable_activation/activation_type changes at any cycle, and from the first clock edge. OPMODE is always one of two legal encodings, so P is never X.

Edits for the editor (module sub_activation, lines 8466-8647; nothing else in the file changes)

  1. Delete the declarations of slope_applied_data_internal (8480) and intercept_applied_data_internal (8481) and every assignment to them: lines 8498, 8499, 8509, 8511, 8545, 8546. Keep the rest of that always block byte-for-byte — relu_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_delayed, out_data_available_internal, cycle_count, activation_in_progress keep their current branches, including the if(activation_type==1'b1) branch that now only assigns data_intercept_delayed.

  2. Add, in the module body:

    • wire act_clr = (reset | ~enable_activation) | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress);
    • wire act_tanh = (activation_type == 1'b1);
    • wire [47:0] act_p;
    • wire [8:0] act_opmode = act_tanh ? 9'b000110101 : 9'b010000000;
    • one instance:
      dsp48e2_macc u_act_macc (.CLK(clk), .A({19'b0, data_slope}), .B({10'b0, inp_data}), .C({40'b0, data_intercept_delayed}), .OPMODE(act_opmode), .RSTA(act_clr), .RSTB(act_clr), .RSTP(act_clr), .CEA2(act_tanh), .CEB2(act_tanh), .CEP(1'b1), .P(act_p));
    • every input port is connected; no reset or enable is left out.
  3. Line 8557 becomes out_data_internal <= act_p[7:0]; (the else branch at 8559 keeps relu_applied_data_internal).

Because sub_activation is instantiated 16 times inside activation, this one edit adds 16 blocks, one per instance, each occupying a DSP tile alone. Tile budget: the 289 mult_9x9 cells currently sit on 112 tiles and the one two_mult_18x19 on 1; removing these 16 cells from that pool cannot increase it, so the total is at most 113 - (tiles freed) + 16 ≤ 129 of the device's 132 DSP tiles.

Register stages, before and after (converted region)

Before, tanh path: inp_data (cycle n) → combinational address decode + slope LUT → stage 1 slope_applied_data_internal (edge n+1) → 8-bit add with data_intercept_delayed → stage 2 intercept_applied_data_internal (edge n+2) → stage 3 out_data_internal (edge n+3) → out_data.
After: inp_data (cycle n) → decode + slope LUT → stage 1 block registers A2/B2 (edge n+1) → block multiply + C adder → stage 2 block register P (edge n+2) → stage 3 soft out_data_internal (edge n+3) → out_data. Three stages before and after, same cycle on every output; data_intercept_delayed keeps its single soft stage and reaches P through the unregistered C port in one cycle, as the original adder did.

Candidates left, with the reason

  • qmult.$mul$...:4969$853 (256 instances): not convertible. Each of the 256 processing elements multiplies every cycle, so the rewrite would need 256 blocks and the device has 132 DSP tiles. Also, the one register stage available (a_flopped/b_flopped) is the only one between the operands and out_c: the product is consumed combinationally by the saturating downcast (4937-4949) and qadd (4954), whose result leaves the module in the same cycle, so the block's mandatory P register would add a cycle that nothing downstream can absorb.
  • pooling.$mul$...:7612$1133 (matrix_size_int*y): not convertible. The product is combinational into raddr_accum0_pool, an output port read in the same cycle (7612) — there is no register after it to absorb P. Feeding the block two cycles early is impossible: y's next-next value depends on start_pooling and resetn one cycle in the future (7614-7632). Maintaining matrix_size_int*y as a running accumulation in P would be exact only while matrix_size/enable_pool are constant during pooling, and the reference testbench drives the APB port with random addresses and data throughout (set_data, rand_cycles), so matrix_size_int changes mid-run and the accumulation would diverge.
  • pooling.$mul$...:8143$1656 (filter_size_int*filter_size_int): not convertible, same reason — after synthesis this one cell feeds the comparators at 7495, 7607 and 8143 combinationally in the cycle filter_size_int is presented; the block's two-cycle A/B path would use a stale filter_size_int for two cycles after each APB write to REG_POOL_WINDOW_ADDR, which the testbench's random APB traffic performs.
  • norm_sub.$mul$...:5632$876 (16 instances): left, not chosen this pass (a correct rewrite exists only with a change outside the region). variance_applied_data[n+1] = mean_applied_data[n] * inv_var[n] is one register stage, while the block needs two (A2/B2 then P). mean_applied_data can be fed one cycle early (its next value, validity_mask ? inp_data-mean : inp_data, is available combinationally, and the register must stay in soft logic anyway to drive C for the validity_mask==0 pass-through via OPMODE 000110000), but inv_var cannot: it is a cfg register (5012) that the random APB stimulus rewrites at arbitrary cycles, so B2 would hold a one-cycle-stale inv_var and mismatch. Making it exact requires cfg to export inv_var's next-state value, which is outside this region; it is also the least profitable conversion (it removes only the multiply and one 8-bit mux, and costs 16 more DSP tiles).

DESIGN_CHANGE: Two changes would widen what can use the block. (1) If cfg exported the next-state value of inv_var (or norm_sub registered inv_var one cycle before use, as it already does for mean_applied_data), the 16 norm_sub variance multiplies would map onto the block exactly. (2) In pooling, if raddr_accum0_pool and pool_norm_valid were allowed to be one cycle later than the counters that feed them (i.e. the address and the pool_count comparisons were registered), both pooling multiplies could move onto the block; as written both products are consumed combinationally in the cycle their operands change. The 256 PE multiplies cannot use this block under any design change that keeps one multiplier per PE, since 256 exceeds the 132 DSP tiles on the device.

1	// dsp48e2_macc: behavioural model of the arch mode dsp48e2_macc_mode, which mimics the AMD DSP48E2 slice in the
2	// one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" operation mode (UG579 v1.7 Table 2-12), attributes AREG = BREG = 1,
3	// ACASCREG = BCASCREG = 1, MREG = 0, PREG = 1, CREG = 0, OPMODEREG = 0, USE_MULT = MULTIPLY, AMULTSEL = A,
4	// BMULTSEL = B, no pre-adder, no cascade, no pattern detector, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000,
5	// CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
6	// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSP48E2.v (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the
7	// initial block at lines 1178-1217), read in place and never copied.
8	// Per clock edge: A2 <= A if CEA2 (cleared by RSTA); B2 <= B if CEB2 (cleared by RSTB);
9	// P <= f(OPMODE, A2*B2, C, P) if CEP (cleared by RSTP). A, B to P: 2 cycles; C and OPMODE to P: 1 cycle
10	// (they are not registered). P wraps at 48 bits. OPMODE is restricted to the eight values below; on
11	// any other value at an edge that loads P, P becomes X (the mode does not define it).
12	// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
13	// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
14	// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
15	// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are
16	// this project's arch's own, not AMD's. One block per DSP tile.
17	module dsp48e2_macc (
18	    input CLK,
19	    input [26:0] A,
20	    input [17:0] B,
21	    input [47:0] C,
22	    input [8:0] OPMODE,
23	    input RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2, CEP,
24	    output [47:0] P
25	);
26	    reg [26:0] a2 = 0;
27	    reg [17:0] b2 = 0;
28	    reg [47:0] p = 0;
29	    wire signed [44:0] m = $signed(a2) * $signed(b2);       // signed 27x18 -> 45, exact
30	    wire [47:0] mx = {{3{m[44]}}, m};
31	    // what P loads, as a function so that it is defined from time 0
32	    function [47:0] alu;
33	        input [8:0] opmode;
34	        input [47:0] p;
35	        input [47:0] C;
36	        input [47:0] mx;
37	        case (opmode)
38	            9'b000000101: alu = mx;            // multiply
39	            9'b000100101: alu = p + mx;        // multiply-accumulate (Z = P)
40	            9'b000110101: alu = C + mx;        // multiply-add (Z = C)
41	            9'b010110101: alu = p + C + mx;    // multiply-add with P (W = P, Z = C)
42	            9'b110000000: alu = C;             // load C (W = C)
43	            9'b000110000: alu = C;             // load C (Z = C)
44	            9'b010000000: alu = p;             // hold through the ALU (W = P)
45	            9'b000000000: alu = 48'd0;         // zero
46	            default:      alu = {48{1'bx}};    // not a value of this mode
47	        endcase
48	    endfunction
49	    wire [47:0] nxt = alu(OPMODE, p, C, mx);
50	    always @(posedge CLK) begin
51	        if (RSTA) a2 <= 0; else if (CEA2) a2 <= A;
52	        if (RSTB) b2 <= 0; else if (CEB2) b2 <= B;
53	        if (RSTP) p <= 0; else if (CEP) p <= nxt;
54	    end
55	    assign P = p;
56	endmodule

5596	module norm_sub(
5597		input enable_norm,
5598	    input [`DWIDTH-1:0] mean,
5599	    input [`DWIDTH-1:0] inv_var,
5600	    input in_data_available,
5601	    input [`DWIDTH-1:0] inp_data,
5602	    output [`DWIDTH-1:0] out_data,
5603	    output out_data_available,
5604	    input  validity_mask,
5605	    input clk,
5606	    input reset
5607	);
5608	
5609	reg out_data_available_internal;
5610	wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] out_data_internal;
5611	reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] mean_applied_data;
5612	reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] variance_applied_data;
5613	reg norm_in_progress;
5614	
5615	//Muxing logic to handle the case when this block is disabled
5616	assign out_data_available = (enable_norm) ? out_data_available_internal : in_data_available;
5617	assign out_data = (enable_norm) ? out_data_internal : inp_data;
5618	
5619	always @(posedge clk) begin
5620	    if ((reset || ~enable_norm)) begin
5621	        mean_applied_data <= 0;
5622	        variance_applied_data <= 0;
5623	    end else if (in_data_available||norm_in_progress) begin
5624	        //Let's apply mean and variance as the input data comes in.
5625	        //We have a pipeline here. First stage does the add (to apply the mean)
5626	        //and second stage does the multiplication (to apply the variance).
5627	        //Note: the following loop is not a loop across multiple columns of data.
5628	        //This loop will run in 2 cycle on the same column of data that comes into 
5629	        //this module in 1 clock.
5630	        if (validity_mask == 1'b1) begin
5631	            mean_applied_data <= (inp_data - mean);
5632	            variance_applied_data <= (mean_applied_data * inv_var);
5633	        end 
5634	        else begin
5635	            mean_applied_data <= (inp_data);
5636	            variance_applied_data <= (mean_applied_data);
5637	        end
5638	    end
5639	    else begin
5640	        mean_applied_data <= 0;
5641	        variance_applied_data <= 0;
5642	    end
5643	end
5644	
5645	//The data is normalized in two cycles so we are shifting in_data_available by 2 to generate out_data_available
5646	always @(posedge clk) begin
5647		norm_in_progress <= in_data_available;
5648		out_data_available_internal <= norm_in_progress;
5649	end
5650	
5651	assign out_data_internal = variance_applied_data;
5652	
5653	endmodule

8503	      cycle_count                 <= 0;
8504	      activation_in_progress      <= 0;      
8505	   end 
8506	   else if(in_data_available || activation_in_progress) begin
8507	      cycle_count <= cycle_count + 1;
8508	      if(activation_type==1'b1) begin // tanH
8509	        slope_applied_data_internal <= data_slope * inp_data;
8510	        data_intercept_delayed <= data_intercept;
8511	        intercept_applied_data_internal <= slope_applied_data_internal + data_intercept_delayed;
8512	      end else begin // ReLU
8513	        relu_applied_data_internal <= (inp_data)? {`DWIDTH{1'b0}} : inp_data;
8514	      end 
8515	      
8516	      //TANH needs 1 extra cycle
8517	      if (activation_type==1'b1) begin
8518	         if (cycle_count==2) begin
8519	            out_data_available_internal <= 1;
8520	         end
8521	      end else begin
8522	         if (cycle_count==1) begin
8523	           out_data_available_internal <= 1;
8524	         end
8525	      end
8526	
8527	      //TANH needs 1 extra cycle
8528	      if (activation_type==1'b1) begin
8529	        if(cycle_count==2) begin
8530	           activation_in_progress <= 0;
8531	        end
8532	        else begin
8533	           activation_in_progress <= 1;
8534	        end
8535	      end else begin
8536	        if(cycle_count==1) begin
8537	           activation_in_progress <= 0;
8538	        end
8539	        else begin
8540	           activation_in_progress <= 1;
8541	        end
8542	      end
8543	   end   
8544	   else begin
8545	      slope_applied_data_internal     <= 0;
8546	      intercept_applied_data_internal <= 0; 
8547	      relu_applied_data_internal      <= 0; 
8548	      data_intercept_delayed      <= 0;
8549	      out_data_available_internal <= 0;
8550	      cycle_count                 <= 0;
8551	      activation_in_progress      <= 0;
8552	   end
8553	end
8554	
8555	always @ (posedge clk) begin
8556	   if (activation_type == 1'b1)
8557	      out_data_internal <= intercept_applied_data_internal;
8558	   else
8559	      out_data_internal <= relu_applied_data_internal;
8560	end
8561	
8562	//Our equation of tanh is Y=AX+B
8563	//A is the slope and B is the intercept.
8564	//We store A in one LUT and B in another.
8565	//LUT for the slope
8566	always @(address) begin
8567	    case (address)
8568	      4'b0000: data_slope = 8'd0;

8570	      4'b0010: data_slope = 8'd2;
8571	      4'b0011: data_slope = 8'd3;
8572	      4'b0100: data_slope = 8'd4;
8573	      4'b0101: data_slope = 8'd0;
8574	      4'b0110: data_slope = 8'd4;
8575	      4'b0111: data_slope = 8'd3;

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, "27x18 + C MULT/MACC, one slice" (UG579 v1.7 Table 2-12 (p44); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p28)). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 p36: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. P loads the value OPMODE selects from A2*B2, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. The block has no path around a register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+1); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= A2*B2
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + A2*B2
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + A2*B2
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + A2*B2
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2 and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at A, B, RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2 and CEP: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at C and OPMODE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is tpu_like.small.ws.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

Multiplies in the unmodified tpu_like.small.ws, counted in its packed netlist at seed 1: 290 on 113 DSP tiles, of which 289 in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode on 112 tiles (44 tiles with 4, 10 with 3, 25 with 2 and 33 with 1) and 1 in two_mult_18x19 mode on 1 tiles (1 to a tile). 50 products in the source become 290 multiply cells after synthesis. The DSP tile count is the same at each of the five seeds. Multiplies in soft logic, if any, are not in this count.

Each row is one multiply of the design as elaborated from the source. An id names it by its module and by the name the elaborated design gives the multiply, which carries the file and the line; multiplies that come from one line (several written on it, or one inside a generate loop) differ in the number after the last $. An operand's bits are the bits it carries, the zero or sign extension to the width of the expression left out; signed or unsigned is how the multiply reads it. Instances is how many instances of the multiply's module the design holds. Nothing else about a multiply is listed: what its product feeds is in the source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2 and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the relevant regions.

REGIONS: sub_activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.ws.v:8509$1681
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 16
MAPPING: one dsp48e2_macc per sub_activation instance (16 instances, all inside module activation): CLK=clk; A={19'b0, data_slope} (8-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 27, MSB 0); B={10'b0, inp_data} (8-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 18, MSB 0); C={40'b0, data_intercept_delayed} (kept as a soft register, unchanged); OPMODE= (activation_type==1'b1) ? 9'b000110101 (C + A2*B2) : 9'b010000000 (hold); RSTA=RSTB=RSTP= act_clr = ((reset | ~enable_activation) | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress)); CEA2=CEB2= activation_type; CEP=1'b1; P[7:0] replaces the register intercept_applied_data_internal (the block's A2/B2 pair replaces the register slope_applied_data_internal).

PLAN:

What is converted and why it is exact

Only the tanh multiply in sub_activation (line 8509) is converted. In that module the product feeds a register and that register feeds an adder into a second register, which is exactly the block's A2/B2-then-P shape:

Design (cycle n values; clr = (reset|~enable_activation) | ~(in_data_available|activation_in_progress), tanh = activation_type==1):

  • slope_applied_data_internal[n+1] = clr ? 0 : (tanh ? (data_slope*inp_data) mod 256 : hold) (lines 8498, 8509, 8545)
  • intercept_applied_data_internal[n+1] = clr ? 0 : (tanh ? (slope_applied_data_internal + data_intercept_delayed) mod 256 : hold) (lines 8499, 8511, 8546)

Block, with the mapping above:

  • A2[n+1]/B2[n+1] = clr ? 0 : (tanh ? data_slope/inp_data : hold) — RSTA/RSTB win over CEA2/CEB2, so the three cases match one-for-one. By induction from power-up (both the block's registers and, under the testbench's $init_zero, the design's registers start at 0), (A2*B2) mod 2^8 == slope_applied_data_internal on every cycle; the design truncates the product to 8 bits and only its low 8 bits are ever used downstream, so the full 45-bit product is a legal stand-in.
  • P[n+1] = clr ? 0 (RSTP) : (tanh ? C + A2*B2 : P) — so P[7:0] == intercept_applied_data_internal on every cycle, because the design's add is mod 2^8 and low-8 addition only depends on low-8 operands.

Visibility: slope_applied_data_internal is read only at line 8511 (checked: declarations/uses at 8480, 8498, 8509, 8511, 8545), so it may disappear inside the block as the A2/B2 pair. intercept_applied_data_internal is read only at line 8557, which now reads P[7:0]. data_intercept_delayed stays a soft register (its own update at lines 8501/8510/8548 is untouched) and drives C; C is not registered in the block, so the value at cycle n+1 is the one added at edge n+2 — the same cycle the design uses it.

No assumption is needed: every control signal (reset, enable_activation, in_data_available, activation_in_progress, activation_type) is sampled in the same cycle the original samples it, so the mapping holds after every mid-run reset of reset, after enable_activation/activation_type changes at any cycle, and from the first clock edge. OPMODE is always one of two legal encodings, so P is never X.

Edits for the editor (module sub_activation, lines 8466-8647; nothing else in the file changes)

  1. Delete the declarations of slope_applied_data_internal (8480) and intercept_applied_data_internal (8481) and every assignment to them: lines 8498, 8499, 8509, 8511, 8545, 8546. Keep the rest of that always block byte-for-byte — relu_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_delayed, out_data_available_internal, cycle_count, activation_in_progress keep their current branches, including the if(activation_type==1'b1) branch that now only assigns data_intercept_delayed.

  2. Add, in the module body:

    • wire act_clr = (reset | ~enable_activation) | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress);
    • wire act_tanh = (activation_type == 1'b1);
    • wire [47:0] act_p;
    • wire [8:0] act_opmode = act_tanh ? 9'b000110101 : 9'b010000000;
    • one instance:
      dsp48e2_macc u_act_macc (.CLK(clk), .A({19'b0, data_slope}), .B({10'b0, inp_data}), .C({40'b0, data_intercept_delayed}), .OPMODE(act_opmode), .RSTA(act_clr), .RSTB(act_clr), .RSTP(act_clr), .CEA2(act_tanh), .CEB2(act_tanh), .CEP(1'b1), .P(act_p));
    • every input port is connected; no reset or enable is left out.
  3. Line 8557 becomes out_data_internal <= act_p[7:0]; (the else branch at 8559 keeps relu_applied_data_internal).

Because sub_activation is instantiated 16 times inside activation, this one edit adds 16 blocks, one per instance, each occupying a DSP tile alone. Tile budget: the 289 mult_9x9 cells currently sit on 112 tiles and the one two_mult_18x19 on 1; removing these 16 cells from that pool cannot increase it, so the total is at most 113 - (tiles freed) + 16 ≤ 129 of the device's 132 DSP tiles.

Register stages, before and after (converted region)

Before, tanh path: inp_data (cycle n) → combinational address decode + slope LUT → stage 1 slope_applied_data_internal (edge n+1) → 8-bit add with data_intercept_delayed → stage 2 intercept_applied_data_internal (edge n+2) → stage 3 out_data_internal (edge n+3) → out_data.
After: inp_data (cycle n) → decode + slope LUT → stage 1 block registers A2/B2 (edge n+1) → block multiply + C adder → stage 2 block register P (edge n+2) → stage 3 soft out_data_internal (edge n+3) → out_data. Three stages before and after, same cycle on every output; data_intercept_delayed keeps its single soft stage and reaches P through the unregistered C port in one cycle, as the original adder did.

Candidates left, with the reason

  • qmult.$mul$...:4969$853 (256 instances): not convertible. Each of the 256 processing elements multiplies every cycle, so the rewrite would need 256 blocks and the device has 132 DSP tiles. Also, the one register stage available (a_flopped/b_flopped) is the only one between the operands and out_c: the product is consumed combinationally by the saturating downcast (4937-4949) and qadd (4954), whose result leaves the module in the same cycle, so the block's mandatory P register would add a cycle that nothing downstream can absorb.
  • pooling.$mul$...:7612$1133 (matrix_size_int*y): not convertible. The product is combinational into raddr_accum0_pool, an output port read in the same cycle (7612) — there is no register after it to absorb P. Feeding the block two cycles early is impossible: y's next-next value depends on start_pooling and resetn one cycle in the future (7614-7632). Maintaining matrix_size_int*y as a running accumulation in P would be exact only while matrix_size/enable_pool are constant during pooling, and the reference testbench drives the APB port with random addresses and data throughout (set_data, rand_cycles), so matrix_size_int changes mid-run and the accumulation would diverge.
  • pooling.$mul$...:8143$1656 (filter_size_int*filter_size_int): not convertible, same reason — after synthesis this one cell feeds the comparators at 7495, 7607 and 8143 combinationally in the cycle filter_size_int is presented; the block's two-cycle A/B path would use a stale filter_size_int for two cycles after each APB write to REG_POOL_WINDOW_ADDR, which the testbench's random APB traffic performs.
  • norm_sub.$mul$...:5632$876 (16 instances): left, not chosen this pass (a correct rewrite exists only with a change outside the region). variance_applied_data[n+1] = mean_applied_data[n] * inv_var[n] is one register stage, while the block needs two (A2/B2 then P). mean_applied_data can be fed one cycle early (its next value, validity_mask ? inp_data-mean : inp_data, is available combinationally, and the register must stay in soft logic anyway to drive C for the validity_mask==0 pass-through via OPMODE 000110000), but inv_var cannot: it is a cfg register (5012) that the random APB stimulus rewrites at arbitrary cycles, so B2 would hold a one-cycle-stale inv_var and mismatch. Making it exact requires cfg to export inv_var's next-state value, which is outside this region; it is also the least profitable conversion (it removes only the multiply and one 8-bit mux, and costs 16 more DSP tiles).

DESIGN_CHANGE: Two changes would widen what can use the block. (1) If cfg exported the next-state value of inv_var (or norm_sub registered inv_var one cycle before use, as it already does for mean_applied_data), the 16 norm_sub variance multiplies would map onto the block exactly. (2) In pooling, if raddr_accum0_pool and pool_norm_valid were allowed to be one cycle later than the counters that feed them (i.e. the address and the pool_count comparisons were registered), both pooling multiplies could move onto the block; as written both products are consumed combinationally in the cycle their operands change. The 256 PE multiplies cannot use this block under any design change that keeps one multiplier per PE, since 256 exceeds the 132 DSP tiles on the device.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_ws_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (dsp48e2_macc.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: tpu_like.small.ws.v

response

I'll read the design source, focusing on the sub_activation module and the other candidate regions.

APPROVE

Verified against tpu_like.small.ws.v and sim_reference/dsp48e2_macc.v:

  • Converted region (sub_activation, line 8509). The three-way update of slope_applied_data_internal (8498 clear / 8509 load under activation_type==1 / 8545 clear, hold otherwise) maps exactly onto RSTA/RSTB = act_clr winning over CEA2/CEB2 = act_tanh; act_clr = (reset|~enable_activation) | ~(in_data_available|activation_in_progress) matches 8497/8506/8544, including the else clear at 8544-8552 that the plan folds in. intercept_applied_data_internal (8499/8511/8546) maps onto RSTP/CEP=1 with OPMODE = 000110101 (C+A2*B2, matches the model at sim_reference/dsp48e2_macc.v:40) / 010000000 (hold, line 44). Cycle alignment is right: A2/B2 at cycle n+1 carry cycle-n operands, and unregistered C = data_intercept_delayed at cycle n is added at edge n+1, the same pairing as 8511.
  • Visibility. slope_applied_data_internal is read only at 8511; intercept_applied_data_internal only at 8557; data_intercept_delayed only at 8511 and stays a soft register (8501/8510/8548). Confirmed by full-file grep.
  • Widths/arithmetic. DWIDTH=8 (line 9); {19'b0,data_slope}=27, {10'b0,inp_data}=18 with MSB 0, {40'b0,data_intercept_delayed}=48. Only P[7:0] is consumed, so the full 45-bit product and the zero-extended intercept give the same low-8 result as the design's mod-256 truncation at 8509/8511.
  • X-safety. activation_type is a cfg register reset to 1 (5098) and written only from PWDATA[0] (5170), so act_tanh, and hence OPMODE, is never X; OPMODE is always one of two legal encodings.
  • 16 instances confirmed (8258-8453), so 16 blocks; 113 + 16 = 129 ≤ 132 DSP tiles in the worst case.
  • Left candidates. qmult (4969): out is combinational through the downcast (4937-4949) and qadd (4954) with only a_flopped/b_flopped ahead of it (4919-4929) — valid correctness reason, plus 256 > 132 tiles. pooling 7612 and 8143: both products are combinational into raddr_accum0_pool (7612), pool_count0 (7495), pool_norm_valid (7607) and pool15_wire (8143), with matrix_size_int/filter_size_int from enable_pool-gated cfg regs (7486-7489) that the testbench's random APB traffic (drive_tpu_ws_top.vh:34-37) rewrites mid-run — valid. norm_sub (5632): one stage vs. the block's two, and inv_var is a cfg output reg (5012) with no next-state visibility — "not chosen this pass" with a stated correctness obstacle.

5617	assign out_data = (enable_norm) ? out_data_internal : inp_data;

5622	        variance_applied_data <= 0;
5623	    end else if (in_data_available||norm_in_progress) begin
5624	        //Let's apply mean and variance as the input data comes in.
5625	        //We have a pipeline here. First stage does the add (to apply the mean)
5626	        //and second stage does the multiplication (to apply the variance).
5627	        //Note: the following loop is not a loop across multiple columns of data.
5628	        //This loop will run in 2 cycle on the same column of data that comes into 
5629	        //this module in 1 clock.

5636	            variance_applied_data <= (mean_applied_data);

5641	        variance_applied_data <= 0;
5642	    end
5643	end

5648		out_data_available_internal <= norm_in_progress;
5649	end

Read completed /work/sim_reference/dsp48e2_macc.v
input
{
 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/dsp48e2_macc.v"
}
output
1	// dsp48e2_macc: behavioural model of the arch mode dsp48e2_macc_mode, which mimics the AMD DSP48E2 slice in the
2	// one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" operation mode (UG579 v1.7 Table 2-12), attributes AREG = BREG = 1,
3	// ACASCREG = BCASCREG = 1, MREG = 0, PREG = 1, CREG = 0, OPMODEREG = 0, USE_MULT = MULTIPLY, AMULTSEL = A,
4	// BMULTSEL = B, no pre-adder, no cascade, no pattern detector, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000,
5	// CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
6	// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSP48E2.v (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the
7	// initial block at lines 1178-1217), read in place and never copied.
8	// Per clock edge: A2 <= A if CEA2 (cleared by RSTA); B2 <= B if CEB2 (cleared by RSTB);
9	// P <= f(OPMODE, A2*B2, C, P) if CEP (cleared by RSTP). A, B to P: 2 cycles; C and OPMODE to P: 1 cycle
10	// (they are not registered). P wraps at 48 bits. OPMODE is restricted to the eight values below; on
11	// any other value at an edge that loads P, P becomes X (the mode does not define it).
12	// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
13	// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
14	// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
15	// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are
16	// this project's arch's own, not AMD's. One block per DSP tile.
17	module dsp48e2_macc (
18	    input CLK,
19	    input [26:0] A,
20	    input [17:0] B,
21	    input [47:0] C,
22	    input [8:0] OPMODE,
23	    input RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2, CEP,
24	    output [47:0] P
25	);
26	    reg [26:0] a2 = 0;
27	    reg [17:0] b2 = 0;
28	    reg [47:0] p = 0;
29	    wire signed [44:0] m = $signed(a2) * $signed(b2);       // signed 27x18 -> 45, exact
30	    wire [47:0] mx = {{3{m[44]}}, m};
31	    // what P loads, as a function so that it is defined from time 0
32	    function [47:0] alu;
33	        input [8:0] opmode;
34	        input [47:0] p;
35	        input [47:0] C;
36	        input [47:0] mx;
37	        case (opmode)
38	            9'b000000101: alu = mx;            // multiply
39	            9'b000100101: alu = p + mx;        // multiply-accumulate (Z = P)
40	            9'b000110101: alu = C + mx;        // multiply-add (Z = C)
41	            9'b010110101: alu = p + C + mx;    // multiply-add with P (W = P, Z = C)
42	            9'b110000000: alu = C;             // load C (W = C)
43	            9'b000110000: alu = C;             // load C (Z = C)
44	            9'b010000000: alu = p;             // hold through the ALU (W = P)
45	            9'b000000000: alu = 48'd0;         // zero
46	            default:      alu = {48{1'bx}};    // not a value of this mode
47	        endcase
48	    endfunction
49	    wire [47:0] nxt = alu(OPMODE, p, C, mx);
50	    always @(posedge CLK) begin
51	        if (RSTA) a2 <= 0; else if (CEA2) a2 <= A;
52	        if (RSTB) b2 <= 0; else if (CEB2) b2 <= B;
53	        if (RSTP) p <= 0; else if (CEP) p <= nxt;
54	    end
55	    assign P = p;
56	endmodule

Call 3 · editor · anthropic/claude-opus-5 · ok in 40 · out 9954 · reasoning 2387 · cache read 604189 · cache write 33217 · 132.1 s
prompt
Task

Edit tpu_like.small.ws.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: dsp48e2_macc (precision INT27)
  • what it is: this mode copies the AMD DSP48E2 block in one configuration, "27x18 + C MULT/MACC, one slice" (UG579 v1.7 Table 2-12 (p44); OPMODE Tables 2-3 to 2-6 (p28)). It is not the whole DSP48E2: only the ports below exist, and the rest of the real block is fixed inside the mode (ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000, CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0, D = 0, A[29:27] = 0, every cascade input 0, every clock enable and reset not listed as a port 0). Differences from the real block: one slice per tile where AMD's tile has two; A is 27 bits: A[29:27] serve only the A:B path, which is outside the mode; OPMODE is restricted to the listed values; PCOUT, ACOUT, BCOUT, the carry, pattern-detect and XOR outputs are not exposed; timing figures and tile area are this project's, not AMD's.
  • computes: P = A*B + C. That is one of the forms the block takes: OPMODE chooses the form at every clock edge (listed below).
  • input ports: CLK[1] (clock), A[27], B[18], C[48], OPMODE[9], RSTA[1], RSTB[1], RSTP[1], CEA2[1], CEB2[1], CEP[1]
  • output ports: P[48]
  • arithmetic: the block's operands and result are signed two's complement. A design operand narrower than its port must be widened to the port width: zero-extended if the design treats it as unsigned, sign-extended if signed. For unsigned data the top bit of each multiplier operand must be zero (UG579 p36: "The multiplier can emulate unsigned math by setting the MSB of an input operand to zero."). So an unsigned operand may be at most 26 bits on A and 17 bits on B; a wider unsigned operand has no exact representation on this block. C and P are 48-bit values added modulo 2^48; extend a narrower addend to 48 bits by its own signedness. The result wraps at 48 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 48 bits (modulo 2^48), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on CLK, the block has exactly these registers. A2 loads A at a clock edge where CEA2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTA. B2 loads B at a clock edge where CEB2 is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTB. P loads the value OPMODE selects from A2*B2, C and P at a clock edge where CEP is high, and is cleared to 0 by RSTP; the output P is this register. The block has no path around a register.
  • timing: A and B reach P 2 clock edges after they are applied (applied before edge k, in P after edge k+1); C and OPMODE have no register before the one that drives P: the value present at a clock edge is in P after that edge (1 cycle).

  • control OPMODE: a per-cycle input, not registered; it acts at the clock edge that loads P, with the C value present at that edge. Legal values, and what the clock edge then does:

    • 000000101 (multiply): P <= A2*B2
    • 000100101 (multiply-accumulate): P <= P + A2*B2
    • 000110101 (multiply-add): P <= C + A2*B2
    • 010110101 (multiply-add with the running sum): P <= P + C + A2*B2
    • 110000000 (load C): P <= C
    • 000110000 (load C (second encoding)): P <= C
    • 010000000 (hold): P <= P
    • 000000000 (zero): P <= 0
    • any other value: a clock edge that loads P under it leaves P unknown (X); the mode does not define it.
  • resets and clock enables (resets RSTA, RSTB and RSTP; clock enables CEA2, CEB2 and CEP): each reset is synchronous and active high, clears only its own register to 0 at the clock edge that samples it high, and wins over that register's clock enable; each clock enable is active high; its register holds its value at a clock edge that samples the enable low. The block has no other reset and no other enable.
  • power-up: every register of the block starts at 0 (as the vendor's simulation model starts them), so P is defined from the first clock edge with or without a reset.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. A reset the rewrite does not need is tied to 0; a clock enable it does not need is tied to 1; OPMODE may be a constant legal value.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block at A, B, RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2 and CEP: a path ends at a register of the block (setup)0.019
into the block at C and OPMODE, which have no input register: the path runs on through the block's delay to the register behind P (delay + setup)2.140 + 0.019 = 2.159
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of P (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path into C and OPMODE is not cut at the block's pins: it runs through the block to the register behind P, so the logic that drives them and the block's delay are in one stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Multiply in the approved plan: sub_activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.ws.v:8509$1681 (module sub_activation).
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's resets, clock enables or per-cycle control, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's resets (RSTA, RSTB and RSTP), clock enables (CEA2, CEB2 and CEP) and per-cycle control (OPMODE) so that "same behavior" holds after every reset of the design, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds in soft logic start unknown; do not rely on their initial value. The block's own registers start at 0, as its power-up line states.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh tpu_like.small.ws.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the relevant regions.

REGIONS: sub_activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.ws.v:8509$1681
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 16
MAPPING: one dsp48e2_macc per sub_activation instance (16 instances, all inside module activation): CLK=clk; A={19'b0, data_slope} (8-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 27, MSB 0); B={10'b0, inp_data} (8-bit unsigned, zero-extended to 18, MSB 0); C={40'b0, data_intercept_delayed} (kept as a soft register, unchanged); OPMODE= (activation_type==1'b1) ? 9'b000110101 (C + A2*B2) : 9'b010000000 (hold); RSTA=RSTB=RSTP= act_clr = ((reset | ~enable_activation) | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress)); CEA2=CEB2= activation_type; CEP=1'b1; P[7:0] replaces the register intercept_applied_data_internal (the block's A2/B2 pair replaces the register slope_applied_data_internal).

PLAN:

What is converted and why it is exact

Only the tanh multiply in sub_activation (line 8509) is converted. In that module the product feeds a register and that register feeds an adder into a second register, which is exactly the block's A2/B2-then-P shape:

Design (cycle n values; clr = (reset|~enable_activation) | ~(in_data_available|activation_in_progress), tanh = activation_type==1):

  • slope_applied_data_internal[n+1] = clr ? 0 : (tanh ? (data_slope*inp_data) mod 256 : hold) (lines 8498, 8509, 8545)
  • intercept_applied_data_internal[n+1] = clr ? 0 : (tanh ? (slope_applied_data_internal + data_intercept_delayed) mod 256 : hold) (lines 8499, 8511, 8546)

Block, with the mapping above:

  • A2[n+1]/B2[n+1] = clr ? 0 : (tanh ? data_slope/inp_data : hold) — RSTA/RSTB win over CEA2/CEB2, so the three cases match one-for-one. By induction from power-up (both the block's registers and, under the testbench's $init_zero, the design's registers start at 0), (A2*B2) mod 2^8 == slope_applied_data_internal on every cycle; the design truncates the product to 8 bits and only its low 8 bits are ever used downstream, so the full 45-bit product is a legal stand-in.
  • P[n+1] = clr ? 0 (RSTP) : (tanh ? C + A2*B2 : P) — so P[7:0] == intercept_applied_data_internal on every cycle, because the design's add is mod 2^8 and low-8 addition only depends on low-8 operands.

Visibility: slope_applied_data_internal is read only at line 8511 (checked: declarations/uses at 8480, 8498, 8509, 8511, 8545), so it may disappear inside the block as the A2/B2 pair. intercept_applied_data_internal is read only at line 8557, which now reads P[7:0]. data_intercept_delayed stays a soft register (its own update at lines 8501/8510/8548 is untouched) and drives C; C is not registered in the block, so the value at cycle n+1 is the one added at edge n+2 — the same cycle the design uses it.

No assumption is needed: every control signal (reset, enable_activation, in_data_available, activation_in_progress, activation_type) is sampled in the same cycle the original samples it, so the mapping holds after every mid-run reset of reset, after enable_activation/activation_type changes at any cycle, and from the first clock edge. OPMODE is always one of two legal encodings, so P is never X.

Edits for the editor (module sub_activation, lines 8466-8647; nothing else in the file changes)

  1. Delete the declarations of slope_applied_data_internal (8480) and intercept_applied_data_internal (8481) and every assignment to them: lines 8498, 8499, 8509, 8511, 8545, 8546. Keep the rest of that always block byte-for-byte — relu_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_delayed, out_data_available_internal, cycle_count, activation_in_progress keep their current branches, including the if(activation_type==1'b1) branch that now only assigns data_intercept_delayed.

  2. Add, in the module body:

    • wire act_clr = (reset | ~enable_activation) | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress);
    • wire act_tanh = (activation_type == 1'b1);
    • wire [47:0] act_p;
    • wire [8:0] act_opmode = act_tanh ? 9'b000110101 : 9'b010000000;
    • one instance:
      dsp48e2_macc u_act_macc (.CLK(clk), .A({19'b0, data_slope}), .B({10'b0, inp_data}), .C({40'b0, data_intercept_delayed}), .OPMODE(act_opmode), .RSTA(act_clr), .RSTB(act_clr), .RSTP(act_clr), .CEA2(act_tanh), .CEB2(act_tanh), .CEP(1'b1), .P(act_p));
    • every input port is connected; no reset or enable is left out.
  3. Line 8557 becomes out_data_internal <= act_p[7:0]; (the else branch at 8559 keeps relu_applied_data_internal).

Because sub_activation is instantiated 16 times inside activation, this one edit adds 16 blocks, one per instance, each occupying a DSP tile alone. Tile budget: the 289 mult_9x9 cells currently sit on 112 tiles and the one two_mult_18x19 on 1; removing these 16 cells from that pool cannot increase it, so the total is at most 113 - (tiles freed) + 16 ≤ 129 of the device's 132 DSP tiles.

Register stages, before and after (converted region)

Before, tanh path: inp_data (cycle n) → combinational address decode + slope LUT → stage 1 slope_applied_data_internal (edge n+1) → 8-bit add with data_intercept_delayed → stage 2 intercept_applied_data_internal (edge n+2) → stage 3 out_data_internal (edge n+3) → out_data.
After: inp_data (cycle n) → decode + slope LUT → stage 1 block registers A2/B2 (edge n+1) → block multiply + C adder → stage 2 block register P (edge n+2) → stage 3 soft out_data_internal (edge n+3) → out_data. Three stages before and after, same cycle on every output; data_intercept_delayed keeps its single soft stage and reaches P through the unregistered C port in one cycle, as the original adder did.

Candidates left, with the reason

  • qmult.$mul$...:4969$853 (256 instances): not convertible. Each of the 256 processing elements multiplies every cycle, so the rewrite would need 256 blocks and the device has 132 DSP tiles. Also, the one register stage available (a_flopped/b_flopped) is the only one between the operands and out_c: the product is consumed combinationally by the saturating downcast (4937-4949) and qadd (4954), whose result leaves the module in the same cycle, so the block's mandatory P register would add a cycle that nothing downstream can absorb.
  • pooling.$mul$...:7612$1133 (matrix_size_int*y): not convertible. The product is combinational into raddr_accum0_pool, an output port read in the same cycle (7612) — there is no register after it to absorb P. Feeding the block two cycles early is impossible: y's next-next value depends on start_pooling and resetn one cycle in the future (7614-7632). Maintaining matrix_size_int*y as a running accumulation in P would be exact only while matrix_size/enable_pool are constant during pooling, and the reference testbench drives the APB port with random addresses and data throughout (set_data, rand_cycles), so matrix_size_int changes mid-run and the accumulation would diverge.
  • pooling.$mul$...:8143$1656 (filter_size_int*filter_size_int): not convertible, same reason — after synthesis this one cell feeds the comparators at 7495, 7607 and 8143 combinationally in the cycle filter_size_int is presented; the block's two-cycle A/B path would use a stale filter_size_int for two cycles after each APB write to REG_POOL_WINDOW_ADDR, which the testbench's random APB traffic performs.
  • norm_sub.$mul$...:5632$876 (16 instances): left, not chosen this pass (a correct rewrite exists only with a change outside the region). variance_applied_data[n+1] = mean_applied_data[n] * inv_var[n] is one register stage, while the block needs two (A2/B2 then P). mean_applied_data can be fed one cycle early (its next value, validity_mask ? inp_data-mean : inp_data, is available combinationally, and the register must stay in soft logic anyway to drive C for the validity_mask==0 pass-through via OPMODE 000110000), but inv_var cannot: it is a cfg register (5012) that the random APB stimulus rewrites at arbitrary cycles, so B2 would hold a one-cycle-stale inv_var and mismatch. Making it exact requires cfg to export inv_var's next-state value, which is outside this region; it is also the least profitable conversion (it removes only the multiply and one 8-bit mux, and costs 16 more DSP tiles).
Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_ws_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (dsp48e2_macc.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

Read completed /work/sim_reference/dsp48e2_macc.v
input
{
 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/dsp48e2_macc.v"
}
output
1	// dsp48e2_macc: behavioural model of the arch mode dsp48e2_macc_mode, which mimics the AMD DSP48E2 slice in the
2	// one-slice "27x18 + C MULT/MACC" operation mode (UG579 v1.7 Table 2-12), attributes AREG = BREG = 1,
3	// ACASCREG = BCASCREG = 1, MREG = 0, PREG = 1, CREG = 0, OPMODEREG = 0, USE_MULT = MULTIPLY, AMULTSEL = A,
4	// BMULTSEL = B, no pre-adder, no cascade, no pattern detector, ALUMODE = 0000, INMODE = 00000,
5	// CARRYINSEL = 000, CARRYIN = 0. Reference for every per-cycle question: AMD's simulation model
6	// /tools/Xilinx/Vivado/2024.1/data/verilog/src/unisims/DSP48E2.v (Vivado 2024.1; registers start at 0 in the
7	// initial block at lines 1178-1217), read in place and never copied.
8	// Per clock edge: A2 <= A if CEA2 (cleared by RSTA); B2 <= B if CEB2 (cleared by RSTB);
9	// P <= f(OPMODE, A2*B2, C, P) if CEP (cleared by RSTP). A, B to P: 2 cycles; C and OPMODE to P: 1 cycle
10	// (they are not registered). P wraps at 48 bits. OPMODE is restricted to the eight values below; on
11	// any other value at an edge that loads P, P becomes X (the mode does not define it).
12	// Signed two's complement throughout, as the real block; unsigned data must have a zero top bit on
13	// each operand. Every register starts at 0: AMD's simulation model starts its registers at 0 in the
14	// `initial` block(s) cited below (Vivado 2024.1). Resets are synchronous, active high, clear only the
15	// register(s) they name and win over that register's clock enable. Timing figures and tile area are
16	// this project's arch's own, not AMD's. One block per DSP tile.
17	module dsp48e2_macc (
18	    input CLK,
19	    input [26:0] A,
20	    input [17:0] B,
21	    input [47:0] C,
22	    input [8:0] OPMODE,
23	    input RSTA, RSTB, RSTP, CEA2, CEB2, CEP,
24	    output [47:0] P
25	);
26	    reg [26:0] a2 = 0;
27	    reg [17:0] b2 = 0;
28	    reg [47:0] p = 0;
29	    wire signed [44:0] m = $signed(a2) * $signed(b2);       // signed 27x18 -> 45, exact
30	    wire [47:0] mx = {{3{m[44]}}, m};
31	    // what P loads, as a function so that it is defined from time 0
32	    function [47:0] alu;
33	        input [8:0] opmode;
34	        input [47:0] p;
35	        input [47:0] C;
36	        input [47:0] mx;
37	        case (opmode)
38	            9'b000000101: alu = mx;            // multiply
39	            9'b000100101: alu = p + mx;        // multiply-accumulate (Z = P)
40	            9'b000110101: alu = C + mx;        // multiply-add (Z = C)
41	            9'b010110101: alu = p + C + mx;    // multiply-add with P (W = P, Z = C)
42	            9'b110000000: alu = C;             // load C (W = C)
43	            9'b000110000: alu = C;             // load C (Z = C)
44	            9'b010000000: alu = p;             // hold through the ALU (W = P)
45	            9'b000000000: alu = 48'd0;         // zero
46	            default:      alu = {48{1'bx}};    // not a value of this mode
47	        endcase
48	    endfunction
49	    wire [47:0] nxt = alu(OPMODE, p, C, mx);
50	    always @(posedge CLK) begin
51	        if (RSTA) a2 <= 0; else if (CEA2) a2 <= A;
52	        if (RSTB) b2 <= 0; else if (CEB2) b2 <= B;
53	        if (RSTP) p <= 0; else if (CEP) p <= nxt;
54	    end
55	    assign P = p;
56	endmodule
